<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, April 8, 2014 
 
PRESENT: Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; and Jesse 
Thompson, Chairman were present in person. Supervisor Jonathan M. Nez was absent.  
9:05 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Chairman Thompson. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
      CONSENT AGENDA : a)Minutes: March 25, 2014 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 March 2014  
Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, February 2014 and Show Low Precinct #5, March 2014 d)Tax 
exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver e) Conduct board member 
elections for Woodruff Domestic Water Improvement District and Pinetop -Lakeside Sanitary Districts on 
November 4, 2014 and Town of Snowflake on August 24, 2014 f) Two month contract for consultant services for 
tribal liaison in the amount of $1,000 per month plus approved travel expenses g) Letter of support for the Hopi 
Tribe’s new application for the Transportation Investment Generating Economic Recovery (2014 TIGER Grant) 
for the development of the Tawa’ovi Community, Phase I Roadway and Infrastructure Project h) Modification #2 
to Cooperative Law Enforcement Agreement with USDA Forest Service contract #2 -LE-11030121-001 and 
Operating and Financial Plan for $8,800 for law enforcement coopera tive efforts for FY14 i)Application for 
Treasurer’s Deeds for the State of Arizona j) Nomination of Supervisor David Tenney for Bureau of Land 
Management Resource Advisory Council: Supervisor Allen made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign 
the items in the consent agenda as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
      BOARD OF SUPERVISORS: Presentation to Dispatcher Helena Leonard for her contributions to the events on 
January 18, 2014: Item pulled from the agenda. 
HUMAN RESOURCES : Consideration and possible  approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
reviewed the 28 item personnel action report. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the personnel 
actions as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Supervisor Tenney declared a conflict on line 2. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion minus one vote. 
      TREASURER: Consideration and possible approval of Personal Property Tax abatements per A.R.S. § 4219118: 
Treasurer Manny Hernandez presented the item.  He indicated these manufactured homes have no value and some 
have been moved or could not be lo cated. Supervisor Allen made a motion to approve the Personal Property 
Tax abatements per A.R.S.  § 42-19118; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible adoption of a Fair Housing Proclamation declaring April as “Fair Housing 
Month” in Navajo County: Mary Springer presented the item. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion 
to adopt the Fair Housing Proclamation declaring April as “Fair Housing Month” in Navajo County; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-08-2014 
    Page 2 of 3 
 
 
b. Consideration and possible approval of revised Section 13 of Fiscal Policy Manual: Mary Sp ringer 
presented the item. Supervisor Allen made a motion to approve the revised Section 13 of Fiscal Policy 
Manual; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Consideration and possible approval of the purchase of a 2015 GMC Sierra from Hatch Motor Company 
for Bioterrorism Defense in the amount of $33,672.89 : Mary Springer discussed the item. Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to approve the purchase of a 2015 GMC Sierra from Hatch Motor Company for 
Bioterrorism Defense in the amount of $33,672.89 ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
d. Consideration and possible approval of the purchase  of a 2014 Ford Escape from Show Low Ford for 
rotation of motor pool vehicles in the amount of $26,441.01 : Mary Springer discussed the item.  
Supervisor Allen made a motion to approve the purchase of a 2014 Ford Escape from Show Low Ford 
for rotation of motor pool vehicles in the amount of $26,441.01; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B14 -02-010 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt 
Products to Cactus Transport, Inc. and Hawker & Evans Asphalt Company, Inc. effective April 23, 2014: 
Bill Bess presented the item.  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the Contract #B14-02-
010 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt Products to Cactus Transport, Inc . and Hawker & Evans 
Asphalt Company, Inc. effective April 23, 2014; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement Number 9 of the Memorandum of Agreement 
AGN0090001 between Bureau of Indian Affairs, Navajo Region and Navajo County: Bill Bess presented 
the item. Mr. Bess indicated on January 14, 2014 the BOS approved Task Agreement AGN0090001 with 
the BIA and the Chinle Agency Dept. of Transportation. Mr. Bess reported the Chinle agency will provide 
the motor grader and this MOA will be for a 6 month period. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to 
approve the Task Agreement Number 9 of the Memorandum of Agreement AGN0090001 between Bureau 
of Indian Affairs, Navajo Region and Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Tenney  expressed appreciation to Catrina Roe for  the delicious ca kes she makes for the Board 
meetings. Supervisor Tenney reported he attended a 4FRI meeting in Flagstaff where they discussed the Good 
Earth Power contract. He indicated he attended meetings at the legislature regarding the county’s share of lottery 
money, and increased funding for ECO. Supervisor Tenney stated he has participated in discussions regarding 
long term solutions for PILT.  He indicated he will be attending a National Forest Symposium in Portland, Oregon 
on April 21 st and 22 nd.  Supervisor Allen indicated she had meetings with her constituents.  She stated she 
attended a meeting reg arding the Mexican W olf expansion with the AZ Game and F ish Department and 
stakeholders in Phoenix, in trying to come to a consensus . She reported on a conference call  meeting with the 
Fish and Wildlife S ervice. Supervisor Whitesinger indicated Sunrise Park Resort had its closing day on April 
6th.  She stated they didn’t have enough snow this season but she appreciated the efforts of their new manager to 
work with these conditions. Supervisor Whitesinger spoke of wildfire preparedness  meetings with the W hite 
Mountain Apache Tribe and Lakeside Fire.  She reported the White Mountain Youth Corp will come to the county 
complex on June 4th for the groundbreaking of the new walk path. Supervisor Whitesinger indicated she will be 
speaking at the community counseling centers. She invited all to attend the 15th annual Great Fort Apache Heritage 
Celebration on May 10 th to experience Apache history and culture . Chairman Thompson  reported on  his

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-08-2014 
    Page 3 of 3 
 
 
meetings in District II. He expressed appreciation to James  Jayne, Homero Vela and the administration staff in 
finding solutions for  his constituent’s issues and concerns. He indicated he attended meetings regarding the 
Winslow Levee, damaged bridges and flooding issues.  He reported on  a meeting with  the Hopi High School 
students on what Navajo Cou nty stands for and our partnership with  their community. James Jayne reminded 
everyone of the Legal Defender’s grand opening after the Board meeting. Brad Carlyon, County Attorney reported 
that the 5th Annual Victim’s Rights Symposium will be this Friday, April 11th in Snowflake at Northland Pioneer 
College. 
ADJOURN: At 9:53a.m. Supervisor Allen made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
 
 
DATE:  April 22, 2014 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board
